{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LEi25UPw3QdyiNYoprX2gy","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Netsmart Technologies — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-16\n     4→Developer website: https://www.ntst.com/\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→Netsmart Technologies is the dominant health IT vendor in the behavioral health and human services sector. Founded in 1992 (with technology origins dating to 1968 through its 1994 acquisition of Creative Socio-Medics), the company is headquartered in Overland Park, Kansas and has approximately 2,600 employees. Netsmart reports that over 754,000 care providers across the U.S. use its platform technology, including 181 Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinics (CCBHCs) in 37 states. The company was described as the largest IT provider in behavioral healthcare by 2005.\n     9→\n    10→Netsmart has been through several rounds of private equity ownership: Bessemer Venture Partners and Insight Venture Partners (2006), Genstar Capital (2010), GI Partners and Allscripts joint venture ($950M, 2016), and then GI Partners and TA Associates (2018, after buying out Allscripts' stake). As of late 2025, GI Partners and TA Associates were reportedly launching a sale process in early 2026, with the company valued at approximately $5 billion including debt (~$250M EBITDA).\n    11→\n    12→Netsmart's product portfolio spans multiple EHR platforms under the \"CareFabric\" umbrella: **myAvatar** (behavioral health), **myEvolv** (addiction, autism, behavioral health, foster care), **myUnity** (post-acute care — home health, hospice, senior living, skilled nursing), **TheraOffice** (physical therapy), **GEHRIMED** (geriatric care), and **myInsight** (public health). They also offer myHealthPointe (consumer/patient portal), CareConnect (interoperability engine), revenue cycle management, IT managed services, workforce management, telehealth, and Bells AI (ambient clinical documentation assistant). Each of these EHR platforms serves different care communities and has its own ONC certification.\n    13→\n    14→## Product: myAvatar Certified Edition\n    15→\n    16→CHPL IDs: 11575\n    17→\n    18→### What It Is\n    19→\n    20→myAvatar is a behavioral health electronic health record (EHR) specifically designed for organizations providing behavioral health and addictions treatment services in community-based, residential, and inpatient settings. It was the first behavioral health software system to earn both Complete EHR Ambulatory and Complete EMR Inpatient certifications from the Drummond Group.\n    21→\n    22→The certified module (myAvatar Certified Edition, version 5, certified 2024-12-27) is broadly certified across 40+ ONC criteria covering clinical data management (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)–(a)(15); transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(3); patient portal/view-download-transmit (e)(1); public health reporting (f)(1)–(f)(3), (f)(5); FHIR APIs (g)(7), (g)(10); and Direct messaging (h)(1). This is a comprehensive certification profile indicating a full-featured clinical EHR, not a narrow specialty module.\n    23→\n    24→myAvatar is part of Netsmart's broader CareFabric ecosystem, which includes the myHealthPointe patient portal, CareConnect interoperability engine, revenue cycle management services, and the Bells AI documentation assistant. The certified module is the core EHR, but the full product experience includes these surrounding components.\n    25→\n    26→### Users & Market\n    27→\n    28→**Primary users**: Clinicians (psychiatrists, psychologists, therapists, counselors, social workers), nurses, front desk/scheduling staff, billing administrators, pharmacy staff, and executive management.\n    29→\n    30→**Clinical settings**: Community mental health centers, psychiatric hospitals, substance abuse/addiction treatment programs, methadone clinics, CCBHCs, FQHCs with behavioral health programs, residential treatment facilities, autism service providers, foster care organizations, state mental health systems, and managed care organizations.\n    31→\n    32→**Scale**: Netsmart claims over 18,000 customers across its product lines and 754,000+ users. myAvatar is one of several EHR platforms; the specific myAvatar customer count is not broken out, but it is described as the flagship behavioral health product. Netsmart has been ranked #1 EHR vendor for behavioral health by Black Book Research.\n    33→\n    34→**Deployment**: Available as either on-premise or cloud-based (SaaS). The SaaS implementation was recently migrated to AWS.\n    35→\n    36→**Notable customer types**: CCBHCs, state behavioral health agencies (e.g., Sacramento County BHS, Yolo County), and large behavioral health organizations like AltaPointe Health. Customer distribution skews toward Hospital & Health Care (28%), Nonprofit Organization Management (10%), Mental Health Care (8%), and Individual & Family Services (7%).\n    37→\n    38→### Modules & Functionality\n    39→\n    40→myAvatar comprises more than 20 modules (per vendor materials and third-party review sites). The following are documented in vendor feature pages, review sites, and certification materials:\n    41→\n    42→**Clinical Documentation & Records**\n    43→- Patient/client records and demographics\n    44→- Progress notes (clinical documentation)\n    45→- Treatment planning with care team approval and compliance tracking\n    46→- Clinical assessments and screening tools\n    47→- syndromic surveillance, electronic case reporting per certified criteria)\n    86→- Seclusion and restraint documentation and tracking\n    87→\n    88→**Interoperability & Data Exchange**\n    89→- designation\n    94→- Closed-loop referral management\n    95→- Datavant EHR integration available\n    96→\n    97→**Patient/Consumer Portal (myHealthPointe)**\n    98→- Secure access to clinical notes integrating labs/vitals/medications.\n   129→\n   130→**Medication/pharmacy data**: Prescription orders, e-prescribing transactions (via Surescripts), pharmacy verification and dispensing records, and recovery/outcome measures.\n   133→\n   134→**Financial/billing data**: Claims, remittance advice, denial records, eligibility verification, utilization management data, credit card payment records, shadow billing data, and FQHC-specific billing data.\n   135→\n   136→**Scheduling data**: Individual and group appointments, appointment history, reminder records.\n   137→\n   138→**Portal/consumer data**: Patient-reported assessments, secure messages between patients and care teams, consent signatures, personal information updates, medication refill requests.\n   139→\n   140→**Interoperability/exchange data**: Transition of care documents (CCDs), Direct secure messages, FHIR-based data exchanges, referral records, and HIE transaction logs (6.5+ billion transactions processed since 2018 across the Netsmart network).\n   141→\n   142→**Public health reporting data**: Immunization data, syndromic surveillance data, electronic case reports.\n   143→\n   144→**Administrative data**: User access logs, audit trails, consent records, provider permissions.\n   145→\n   146→The vendor website does not mention standalone imaging/radiology capabilities, which is unsurprising for a behavioral health-focused EHR. As of late 2025, GI Partners and TA Associates were reportedly launching a sale process in early 2026, with the company valued at approximately $5 billion including debt (~$250M EBITDA).\n\nNetsmart's product portfolio spans multiple EHR platforms under the \"CareFabric\" umbrella: **myAvatar** (behavioral health), **myEvolv** (addiction, autism, behavioral health, foster care), **myUnity** (post-acute care — home health, hospice, senior living, skilled nursing), **TheraOffice** (physical therapy), **GEHRIMED** (geriatric care), and **myInsight** (public health). It was the first behavioral health software system to earn both Complete EHR Ambulatory and Complete EMR Inpatient certifications from the Drummond Group.\n\nThe certified module (myAvatar Certified Edition, version 5, certified 2024-12-27) is broadly certified across 40+ ONC criteria covering clinical data management (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)–(a)(15); transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(3); patient portal/view-download-transmit (e)(1); public health reporting (f)(1)–(f)(3), (f)(5); FHIR APIs (g)(7), (g)(10); and Direct messaging (h)(1). This is a comprehensive certification profile indicating a full-featured clinical EHR, not a narrow specialty module.\n\nmyAvatar is part of Netsmart's broader CareFabric ecosystem, which includes the myHealthPointe patient portal, CareConnect interoperability engine, revenue cycle management services, and the Bells AI documentation assistant. The certified module is the core EHR, but the full product experience includes these surrounding components.\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Primary users**: Clinicians (psychiatrists, psychologists, therapists, counselors, social workers), nurses, front desk/scheduling staff, billing administrators, pharmacy staff, and executive management.\n\n**Clinical settings**: Community mental health centers, psychiatric hospitals, substance abuse/addiction treatment programs, methadone clinics, CCBHCs, FQHCs with behavioral health programs, residential treatment facilities, autism service providers, foster care organizations, state mental health systems, and managed care organizations.\n\n**Scale**: Netsmart claims over 18,000 customers across its product lines and 754,000+ users. myAvatar is one of several EHR platforms; the specific myAvatar customer count is not broken out, but it is described as the flagship behavioral health product. Netsmart has been ranked #1 EHR vendor for behavioral health by Black Book Research.\n\n**Deployment**: Available as either on-premise or cloud-based (SaaS). The SaaS implementation was recently migrated to AWS.\n\n**Notable customer types**: CCBHCs, state behavioral health agencies (e.g., Sacramento County BHS, Yolo County), and large behavioral health organizations like AltaPointe Health. Customer distribution skews toward Hospital & Health Care (28%), Nonprofit Organization Management (10%), Mental Health Care (8%), and Individual & Family Services (7%).\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nmyAvatar comprises more than 20 modules (per vendor materials and third-party review sites). treatment plans\n- Health Information Exchange (HIE) connectivity via Carequality (4,200+ hospitals, 600,000+ providers)\n- FHIR-based APIs (g)(7) and (g)(10) certified\n- Netsmart QHIN (Qualified * Parses the pdftotext output of \"EHI Export All Tables - September 2023.pdf\"\n * into structured JSON with form, table, and column-level detail.\n *\n * Run: bun run extract-tables.ts\n * Input: ../ehi-export-all-tables.txt (pdftotext output of the PDF)\n * Output: tables.json, coverage-summary.json\n */\n\nimport { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from \"fs\";\nimport { join, dirname } from \"path\";\n\nconst scriptDir = dirname(new URL(import.meta.url).pathname);\nconst inputPath = join(scriptDir, \"..\", \"ehi-export-all-tables.txt\");\nconst outputPath = join(scriptDir, \"tables.json\");\nconst summaryPath = join(scriptDir, \"coverage-summary.json\");\n\ninterface Column {\n  name: string;\n  type: string;\n  max_length: number | null;\n  description: string | null;\n}\n\ninterface TableEntry {\n  form_name: string;\n  table_name: string;\n  schema: string;\n  table_short_name: string;\n  table_description: string | null;\n  columns: Column[];\n}\n\nconst raw = readFileSync(inputPath, \"utf-8\");\nconst lines = raw.split(\"\\n\");\n\nconst tables: TableEntry[] = [];\nlet parseErrors: { line: number; reason: string }[] = [];\n\n// State machine\nlet currentForm: string | null = null;\nlet currentTable: string | null = null;\nlet currentTableDesc: string | null = null;\nlet currentColumns: Column[] = [];\nlet currentColumn: Partial<Column> | null = null;\nlet descriptionLines: string[] = [];\nlet inTOC = true; // Skip the TOC at the start\n\nfunction flushColumn() {\n  if (currentColumn?.name && currentColumn?.type) {\n    const desc = descriptionLines.join(\" \").trim() || null;\n    currentColumns.push({\n      name: currentColumn.name,\n      type: currentColumn.type,\n      max_length: currentColumn.max_length ?? null,\n      description: desc,\n    });\n  }\n  currentColumn = null;\n  descriptionLines = [];\n}\n\nfunction flushTable() {\n  flushColumn();\n  if (currentTable) {\n    const parts = currentTable.split(\".\");\n    tables.push({\n      form_name: currentForm ?? \"\",\n      table_name: currentTable,\n      schema: parts.length > 1 ? parts[0] : \"\",\n      table_short_name: parts.length > 1 ? parts.slice(1).join(\".\") : currentTable,\n      table_description: currentTableDesc,\n      columns: currentColumns,\n    });\n  }\n  currentTable = null;\n  currentTableDesc = null;\n  currentColumns = [];\n}\n\nfor (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {\n  const line = lines[i];\n  const trimmed = line.trim();\n\n  // Skip blank lines and page numbers\n  if (trimmed === \"\" || /^\\d+$/.test(trimmed)) continue;\n\n  // Skip TOC lines (contain ......... page references)\n  if (trimmed.includes(\"..........\")) continue;\n\n  // Detect end of TOC / start of content\n  if (inTOC) {\n    if (trimmed === \"Overview\") {\n      inTOC = false;\n    }\n    continue;\n  }\n\n  // Skip known non-table content\n  if (trimmed === \"Overview\" || trimmed.startsWith(\"Glossary of Terms\")) continue;\n  if (trimmed.startsWith(\"EHI Export: myAvatar\")) continue;\n  if (trimmed.startsWith(\"Last Updated:\")) continue;\n  if (trimmed.startsWith(\"Table of Contents\")) continue;\n\n  // Skip glossary entries (detected by context - they're before the first \"Documentation for form:\")\n  // The glossary is between \"Glossary of Terms\" and the first form entry at line ~2109\n\n  // Form header\n  if (trimmed.startsWith(\"Documentation for form:\")) {\n    flushTable();\n    currentForm = trimmed.replace(\"Documentation for form:\", \"\").trim();\n    continue;\n  }\n\n  // Table name\n  if (trimmed.startsWith(\"Table Name:\")) {\n    // If we already have a table in progress, flush it\n    if (currentTable) {\n      flushTable();\n    }\n    currentTable = trimmed.replace(\"Table Name:\", \"\").trim();\n    continue;\n  }\n\n  // Table description\n  if (trimmed.startsWith(\"Table Description:\")) {\n    currentTableDesc = trimmed.replace(\"Table Description:\", \"\").trim();\n    continue;\n  }\n\n  // Separator line\n  if (trimmed === \"-------------\") continue;\n\n  // Column name\n  if (trimmed.startsWith(\"Column The page is a Netsmart certifications hub covering all their products.\n\n2. **Searched the HTML source** for EHI-related links:\n   ```bash\n   grep -oiE 'href=\"[^\"]*\"' /tmp/ntst-certifications.html | grep -iE 'ehi|export|data.dictionary|b.10|bulk'\n   ```\n   Found a dedicated EHI section with product-specific links. The certifications page has a heading \"EHI (Electronic Health Information) All Data Export\" with links to per-product EHI pages.\n\n3. **Navigated to the myAvatar-specific EHI page**:\n   ```bash\n   curl -sL \"https://www.ntst.com/lp/certifications/ehi-all-data-myavatar\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0'\n   ```\n   This is a dedicated page titled \"EHI (Electronic Health Information) All Data Export for myAvatar\" with explanatory text and a download link.\n\n4. **Downloaded the EHI export documentation ZIP** linked from the page (\"CLICK HERE\"):\n   ```bash\n   curl -sL \"https://www.ntst.com/-/media/pdfs/certifications/EHI_Export_All_Tables_myAvatar_September_2023.zip\" \\\n     -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' \\\n     -o downloads/EHI_Export_All_Tables_myAvatar_September_2023.zip\n   ```\n   File is 12.5 MB, verified as valid ZIP archive. Contains a single PDF: \"EHI Export All Tables - September 2023.pdf\" (16 MB, 7,492 pages).\n\n5. **Extracted and examined the PDF**:\n   ```bash\n   unzip EHI_Export_All_Tables_myAvatar_September_2023.zip\n   pdfinfo \"EHI Export All Tables - September 2023.pdf\"\n   pdftotext \"EHI Export All Tables - September 2023.pdf\" ehi-export-all-tables.txt\n   ```\n   Author: Walz, Dru Anne. Created 2023-10-02 with Microsoft Word. 7,492 pages.\n\n6. **Saved the EHI page HTML** and **took a screenshot** via browser for reference.\n\n7. **Built an enrichment script** (`downloads/enrichment/extract-tables.ts`) to parse the pdftotext output into structured JSON. Run with `bun run extract-tables.ts`.\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### The EHI Export Mechanism\n\nThe EHI page describes the export as:\n\n> \"EHI Export functionality allows organizations to do a **manual one-time export** of health data for one or more patients. This schema is intended for EHI export functionality.\"\n\nThe export produces **computable, delimited files** in a format native to myAvatar. Each database table is exported to its own file with a filename matching the table name. The export covers \"both the standard product forms and tables in myAvatar as well as the tables associated to our integrated applications like Flowsheet.\"\n\nThe page notes that \"some electronic health information might not be available in a table format, such as rich text documents or images. This information is referenced in the extracts created for subsequent export.\" This means rich-text clinical notes and images are handled separately (presumably included by reference).\n\nThe export content varies by organization based on: (1) software applications in use, (2) software version, (3) documentation/software use practices, and (4) configuration decisions and customizations.\n\nThe page also distinguishes this EHI export from their FHIR API and other integration options, explicitly pointing users to the \"Netsmart Developer Portal\" for FHIR API documentation and \"Information Sharing\" for HL7/web service APIs. This is a good sign — they understand the EHI export is a different mechanism from their (g)(10) FHIR API.\n\n### The Data Dictionary (PDF)\n\nThe ZIP contains a single massive PDF: **\"EHI Export All Tables - September 2023.pdf\"** (7,492 pages, last updated 2023-09-27).\n\nStructure:\n- **Table of Contents** (55 pages) listing all forms and tables\n- **Overview** explaining the documentation approach\n- **Glossary of Terms** defining common field patterns (PATID, FACILITY, EPISODE_NUMBER, ss_ fields, JOIN_TO/LINK_TO relationships, etc.)\n- **Form-by-form and table-by-table documentation** comprising the bulk of the document\n\nEach table entry includes:\n- **Form name**: The myAvatar UI form associated with the table\n- **Table name**: Fully qualified as schema.table_name (e.g., `SYSTEM.billing_tx_charge_detail`)\n- **Table description**: Brief explanation of what the table stores\n- **Column definitions**: For each column:\n  - Column name\n  - Column type (varchar, integer, numeric, date, time, timestamp)\n  - Max length (for varchar columns)\n  - Description (present for ~81% of columns)\n\n### Parsed Statistics (from enrichment)\n\n| Metric | Value |\n|--------|-------|\n| Total table entries (form-table pairs) | 906 |\n| Unique tables | 561 |\n| Unique forms | 217 |\n| Total columns across all entries | 71,903 |\n| Columns with descriptions | 58,321 (81.1%) |\n| Columns with max length specified | 61,215 (85.1%) |\n\n**Schema breakdown** (tables by database schema):\n\n| Schema | Table Entries | Purpose |\n|--------|---------------|---------|\n| SYSTEM | 746 | Core myAvatar tables |\n| STATEFORM | 60 | State-specific behavioral health reporting |\n| CWSTEMP | 40 | Clinical Workstation temporary/session tables |\n| OrderEntry | 27 | CPOE and order management |\n| Methadone | 8 | Methadone/MAT-specific medication management |\n| DocR | 7 | Document routing and rules |\n| INCIDENT | 5 | Incident reporting |\n| eMAR | 5 | Electronic medication administration records |\n| LAPROV | 3 | Louisiana provider-specific tables |\n| HL7 | 2 | HL7 integration/external ID mapping |\n| GL | 1 | General ledger |\n| CWSOrderEntry | 1 | Clinical Workstation order entry |\n| CWSSF | 1 | Clinical Workstation state-specific (Florida) |\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThis is one of the most comprehensive EHI export data dictionaries encountered. Netsmart has documented the full database schema across all of myAvatar's modules, not just the clinical summary data that would be covered by a FHIR API.\n\n**Domains clearly covered:**\n\n- **Billing/Financial** (159 unique tables): Claims processing (CMS-1500, UB-04, 837I/P), remittance (835), claim status (276/277), cash posting, charge input, self-pay billing, Medicare pharmacy billing, financial eligibility, payment history, ledger transactions, retroactive payor changes, benefit enrollment (834). This is exceptionally thorough billing coverage.\n\n- **Demographics/Patient** (168 unique tables): Client enrollment, admission/discharge records (including outpatient and backdated), episode management, outreach targets, client merging/purging, pre-admission, program transfers, external client IDs, patient account numbers, and demographic updates.\n\n- **Clinical Notes** (49 unique tables): Progress notes (ambulatory, inpatient, group, individual), co-sign/review workflows, voided notes, scratch notes, service documentation corrections, significant findings. The CWS patient notes tables have multiple supplement tables (supp_1 through supp_6) suggesting extensible note content.\n\n- **Medications/Pharmacy** (31 unique tables): Medication orders (including quick orders), eMAR administration records, NCPDP claim submission/response, methadone-specific medication orders (client, recurring, and fixed orders), medication reconciliation, and medication unique tables): Treatment plans (with goals, level of care, recovery plans, todos), care pathway enrollment, clinical reconciliation, assessment-problem planning, and discharge summaries.\n\n- (37 unique tables): Appointment data, series, waiting room, check-in/check-out, telehealth appointments, overbooking, front desk operations.\n\n- **Vitals/Observations** (6 unique tables): Vital signs (including site-specific configurations), observation details and archives.\n\n- **Allergies** (5 unique tables): Client allergies, clinical info, allergy history, and CCD allergy staging.\n\n- scores, subdomain scores), incident data, leave management, seclusion/restraint tracking.\n\n- **Service Authorization** (10 unique tables): Funding source authorizations, member authorizations, This may be captured within the medication order tables or handled by the external Surescripts integration rather than stored natively in myAvatar.\n\n- **Secure messaging content** (patient portal messages): The myHealthPointe portal has its own separate EHI export page, and portal-originated data may not appear in the myAvatar export. This is a reasonable separation given they are separately certified products.\n\n- **Scanned documents and images**: The page explicitly notes these \"might not be available in a table format\" but are \"referenced in the extracts.\" This means the export includes metadata/references to these documents but the actual binary content may require separate handling.\n\n- **Ambient documentation (Bells AI)**: As a newer AI feature, transcripts or AI-generated note drafts may not be captured in the September 2023 documentation.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a **proprietary delimited file format** native to myAvatar — each table exported as its own file. This is **not FHIR, not C-CDA, not a recognized standard interchange format**. It is essentially a database dump in delimited format.\n\nThis is actually appropriate for a (b)(10) export from a behavioral health EHR. myAvatar stores far more data (state-specific forms, methadone management, acuity scoring, seclusion/restraint tracking, 42 CFR Part 2 consent management) than could be represented in FHIR US Core or C-CDA. A database-level export preserves all this data without lossy transformation.\n\nThe export includes:\n- **Relationships between tables** via JOIN_TO and LINK_TO columns (documented in the glossary)\n- **Patient identity** via PATID (medical record number/client ID)\n- **Episode context** via EPISODE_NUMBER\n- **Facility/site context** via FACILITY and SITEID\n- **Audit metadata** (data_entry_date, data_entry_by, UTC timestamps, timezone info)\n- **Coded values** with both codes and their descriptions (e.g., TYPE_OF_TRANSACTION_CODE + type_of_transaction_value)\n\nA third party could reconstruct the patient record from this export, though they would need to understand the myAvatar data model. The glossary and table descriptions provide a reasonable starting point, but there is no formal schema file (no XSD, JSON Schema, or DDL).\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- Extraordinarily comprehensive scope — 561 unique tables, 71,903 columns\n- The document covers the actual database schema, not a marketing summary\n- 81.1% of columns have descriptions, which is good for a document this size\n- The glossary explains common field patterns and relationship conventions\n- Table descriptions explain the purpose of each table\n- Organized by form, which maps to user-facing functionality\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- **No sample data or worked examples**: There are no example export files, sample records, or illustrations of what the delimited output looks like\n- **No file format specification**: The page says \"computable, delimited file format\" but doesn't specify the delimiter, encoding, escaping rules, null handling, or header format\n- **No schema files**: No machine-readable schema (DDL, XSD, JSON Schema). The only structured representation is the PDF itself, which required custom extraction\n- **~19% of columns lack descriptions**: While 81.1% coverage is good, nearly 13,600 columns have no description\n- **Documentation is dated September 2023**: Over 2 years old at time of collection. The product version certified in December 2024 may have additional tables or modified schemas\n- **Rich text and images not fully documented**: The export acknowledges these exist but doesn't specify how they're referenced or exported\n- **No relationship diagram or ERD**: With 561 tables and JOIN_TO/LINK_TO relationships, a formal relationship diagram would greatly aid comprehension\n- **Value set documentation is sparse**: Coded fields reference code/value pairs but the actual allowed values are not enumerated\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**Granularity**: Field-level documentation with column names, data types, max lengths, and descriptions. This is granular enough to understand the structure but lacks cardinality, nullability, primary/foreign key designations, and index information.\n\n**Coded fields**: The pattern of xxx_CODE + xxx_value pairs is documented in the glossary, and descriptions often say \"See xxx_CODE for more information.\" However, the actual valid code values are not enumerated anywhere in the documentation.\n\n**Relationships**: Documented via JOIN_TO_xxx and LINK_TO_xxx column naming conventions. The glossary explains these are used for joins to parent/child documents, but there is no comprehensive relationship map.\n\n**Versioning**: The document is a point-in-time snapshot (September 2023). There is no change history or version comparison.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nNetsmart has done genuine (b)(10) work with myAvatar. Each database table is exported to its own file with a filename matching the table name. The export covers \"both the standard product forms and tables in myAvatar as well as the tables associated to our integrated applications like Flowsheet.\"\n\nThe page notes that \"some electronic health information might not be available in a table format, such as rich text documents or images. This information is referenced in the extracts created for subsequent export.\" This means rich-text clinical notes and images are handled separately (presumably included by reference).\n\nThe export content varies by organization based on: (1) software applications in use, (2) software version, (3) documentation/software use practices, and (4) configuration decisions and customizations.\n\nThe page also distinguishes this EHI export from their FHIR API and other integration options, explicitly pointing users to the \"Netsmart Developer Portal\" for FHIR API documentation and \"Information Sharing\" for HL7/web service APIs.
